    Books / Online Learning Book / Projected OGD: Orabona Chapter 2

    Teaching chapter 11 of 40 · Canonical route compiled

    Projected OGD: Orabona Chapter 2

    The same causal projected algorithm with repaired arbitrary-open source regularity, sharp fixed/decreasing-step regret and fixed-horizon tuning.

    How to read the status. It describes this page's canonical local Lean route, not completion of the cited textbook chapter or every extension listed below.

    01 · OrientWhat you need18 learning goals and the intended reader. 02 · LearnAlgorithm 2.1 with fixed or positive nonincreasing stepsSection 2.1.2; Algorithm 2.1, Proposition 2.11, Lemma 2.12, Theorem 2.13 fixed and decreasing steps, Eq. (2.1) · printed pp. 12-15; PDF pp. 24-27. 20 source theorem restatements, with assumptions and original links. Includes a worked example. 03 · VerifyMathematics ↔ LeanRead intuition and proof structure, then open the exact compiled declaration.

    Orientation

    Who should read this. Readers of convex optimization and online learning; this route shares the canonical Lean declaration graph.

    Learning goals

    • Follow the actual projected recurrence and strict-prefix information flow.
    • Keep the negative terminal residual for fixed and decreasing learning rates.
    • Distinguish fixed-step unbounded-domain analysis from diameter-based tuning.
    • Distinguish arbitrary open differentiability neighborhoods from the retained stronger convex-neighborhood predicate.
    • Follow a distinct current-before-prediction affine prescient path, and retain actual movement energy rather than assuming constrained gradient-energy equality.
    • Derive a nonsmooth convex-loss comparison from an actual proximal minimum by differentiating only the perturbation along a feasible segment.
    • Derive canonical Bregman three-point/nonnegative/gradient identities and a nonsmooth proximal one-step comparison with both negative residuals.
    • Derive feasible finite-part convexity, the actual extended proximal minimum equivalence and the signed one-step comparison.
    • Trace a genuine current-loss partial argmin recursion, its exact failure and prefix semantics, and its actual signed one-step bound.
    • Derive sharp fixed and decreasing-step cumulative regret from the same actual prescient updates, with correct previous-state maximum and negative movement terms.
    • Derive properness and uniqueness from source hypotheses, identify the actual source argmin run, and obtain both printed signed prescient regret bounds.
    • Derive the unbounded polynomially decreasing-step OSD failure from its actual recursion, including phi range/left limit and the exact source horizon threshold.
    • Construct the generic strict-past FTL partial minimizer rule with feasible initialization, exact failure semantics and restricted-domain prefix causality.
    • Compare a cumulative right-endpoint weighted sum with its integral, retaining zero increments and the initial offset.
    • Telescope cumulative energy sums, retaining zero prefixes, empty horizons and zero external scaling.
    • Derive actual adaptive projected-subgradient regret from causal state, zero-feedback skips, weighted potentials and cumulative energy.
    • Separate the source Theorem4.14 regret guarantee from the infimum/attainment correction in its scalar benchmark display.
    • Explain the exact weighted-distance equality, its interior reciprocal-step correction and terminal residual.

    Textbook crosswalk

    Read the mathematics before the Lean interface

    This reading follows Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization. Teaching routes group mathematical dependencies across the shared library. Visible page labels use the cited source's numbered pages; source buttons use the PDF viewer's physical page index, including front matter. Companion papers cover algorithm-specific results.

    Primary spine · arXiv:1912.13213v10, 21 June 2026

    Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization

    Francesco Orabona

    Location
    Section 2.1.2; Algorithm 2.1, Proposition 2.11, Lemma 2.12, Theorem 2.13 fixed and decreasing steps, Eq. (2.1)
    Pages
    printed pp. 12-15; PDF pp. 24-27
    Open at the cited pages ↗
    Reader checkpoint

    Notation you will meet

    x_t
    Point output before observing loss t; Lean iterate at t-1.
    g_t
    Gradient of the supplied ambient real extension of the current loss at x_t. On a thin V its restriction alone does not fix the full ambient gradient; extension independence is not claimed.
    D, G
    A priori positive diameter and gradient norm upper bounds; the source calls G by L.
    projected online gradient descent · ordered flow

    Algorithm 2.1 with fixed or positive nonincreasing steps

    Read top to bottom: each step supplies the state or proof fact used by the next one.

    1. 01
      Initialize

      Choose x1 in V. Use a fixed positive eta, or a prescribed positive nonincreasing schedule eta_t for a bounded V.

    2. 02
      Predict

      Output the current point before seeing the current loss.

    3. 03
      Read current feedback

      Evaluate the current loss gradient at the current point.

    4. 04
      Project

      Project x_t - eta_t times the current gradient onto V and continue. The fixed-step branch uses the same eta throughout.

    Worked example · follow one decision

    Two rounds with an active projection

    Use V=[0,1], x1=0, eta=1 and losses -2x followed by x/2. This is the exact public Lean canary.

    1. 01
      Project the first gradient step

      The first gradient is -2. The unconstrained next point is 2, so projection changes it to 1.

      Worked example step 1. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: The first point is zero, the unconstrained step is two, and the projected point is one.\[x_2=\Pi_{[0,1]}(0-1(-2))=1.\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    2. 02
      Use the changed current loss

      The second gradient is one half. Its update stays inside the interval.

      Worked example step 2. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: The terminal point is one half.\[x_3=\Pi_{[0,1]}(1-1/2)=1/2.\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    3. 03
      Keep a nonzero residual

      Against comparator zero, regret and terminal squared distance are both positive.

      Worked example step 3. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Regret is one half and terminal squared distance is one quarter.\[R_2(0)=1/2,\qquad\|x_3-0\|^2=1/4.\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    Takeaway. Both the projection and the terminal residual matter in a changing-loss instance.

    Scope. This exact-real example is kernel-checked in Tests.OnlineGradientDescentCanary; it is not a numerical experiment. The tuned-rate test uses its own specified trajectory. The repaired endpoints are instantiated in Tests.OnlineGradientDescentSourceCanary. A separate exact plane canary uses V={(x,0)}, f(x,y)=max(exp(-x),y), U={y<exp(-x)}. It proves U open/nonconvex, V unbounded, actual gradient(-1,0) at0 and next(1,0) for eta1, positive regret 1-exp(-2) against(2,0), and terminal square1. This is not a Lean proof that every alternative convex differentiability neighborhood fails. The horizon-tuned interval bound does not claim each tuned iterate clips.

    Source theorem · faithful restatement

    Proposition 2.11: projection decreases distance

    Original at printed pp. 12-15 ↗
    Compiled local source-repair candidateCompiled local OGD source-repair candidate, with distinct source/body review. Full harness, actual graph, shared Book registry, final reader/site and PR gates remain separate. This is not all Chapter2 or the whole book, and is not merged or live.

    Projection cannot increase distance to a feasible comparator.

    Model
    Projection onto a nonempty closed convex set in real Euclidean space; Lean supports complete real Hilbert spaces.
    Assumptions
    Nonempty closed convex V; feasible comparator.
    Algorithm parameters
    Arbitrary ambient point z and feasible comparator u; no loss, learning rate or horizon.
    Regret notion
    Pointwise comparison of distances; no regret functional.
    Guarantee
    Projection cannot increase distance to a feasible comparator.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Projection cannot increase distance to a feasible comparator.\[\|\Pi_V(z)-u\|\le\|z-u\|\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Exact shared declaration: BanditRL.OnlineGradientDescent.proposition_2_11. Use the Hilbert projection variational inequality and expand squared distance.

    The mathematical content is restated in this site's notation; wording is ours. See printed pp. 12-15 in the linked source for the original statement and full assumptions.

    Source theorem · faithful restatement

    Lemma 2.12: one-step regret

    Original at printed pp. 12-15 ↗
    Compiled local source-repair candidateCompiled local OGD source-repair candidate, with distinct source/body review. Full harness, actual graph, shared Book registry, final reader/site and PR gates remain separate. This is not all Chapter2 or the whole book, and is not merged or live.

    Convexity linearizes loss; projection turns that inner product into a potential decrease plus gradient energy.

    Model
    Finite-dimensional real Euclidean projected OGD; Lean additionally supports real Hilbert spaces.
    Assumptions
    Nonempty closed convex V, feasible x and u, eta>0. Convexity on V and a supplied ambient real extension differentiable on an arbitrary open U containing V. U need not be convex. source_to_feasible proves the conversion to FeasibleRegularLoss: convexity on V and ambient derivatives at every feasible point. On a thin V the supplied extension fixes the gradient; extension independence is not claimed.
    Algorithm parameters
    A single current loss, current feasible point x, feasible comparator u and positive step eta; x_next is the same actual projected update.
    Regret notion
    One-round loss difference and its gradient linearization; no cumulative horizon premise.
    Guarantee
    Convexity linearizes loss; projection turns that inner product into a potential decrease plus gradient energy.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Convexity linearizes loss; projection turns that inner product into a potential decrease plus gradient energy.\[\eta(\ell_t(x_t)-\ell_t(u))\le\eta\langle g_t,x_t-u\rangle\le\frac{\|x_t-u\|^2-\|x_{t+1}-u\|^2+\eta^2\|g_t\|^2}{2}\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Exact shared declaration: BanditRL.OnlineGradientDescentSource.lemma_2_12. Derive the loss comparison from feasible-set convexity and the ambient derivative. Prove the linear gradient, then specialize the retained quadratic projection lemma to the genuine linear loss with this gradient. Both inequalities use the same original step. Convexity on V and a supplied ambient real extension differentiable on an arbitrary open U containing V. U need not be convex. source_to_feasible proves the conversion to FeasibleRegularLoss: convexity on V and ambient derivatives at every feasible point. On a thin V the supplied extension fixes the gradient; extension independence is not claimed.

    The mathematical content is restated in this site's notation; wording is ours. See printed pp. 12-15 in the linked source for the original statement and full assumptions.

    Theorem 2.13: fixed step only

    Original at printed pp. 12-15 ↗
    Compiled local source-repair candidateCompiled local OGD source-repair candidate, with distinct source/body review. Full harness, actual graph, shared Book registry, final reader/site and PR gates remain separate. This is not all Chapter2 or the whole book, and is not merged or live.

    Cumulative comparator regret retains the negative terminal squared distance, with no bounded-domain premise.

    Model
    Finite-dimensional real Euclidean projected OGD; Lean additionally supports real Hilbert spaces.
    Assumptions
    Nonempty closed convex V, feasible initial point and comparator, eta>0; no bounded-domain premise. Convexity on V and a supplied ambient real extension differentiable on an arbitrary open U containing V. U need not be convex. source_to_feasible proves the conversion to FeasibleRegularLoss: convexity on V and ambient derivatives at every feasible point. On a thin V the supplied extension fixes the gradient; extension independence is not claimed.
    Algorithm parameters
    Fixed eta and any natural horizon T, including T=0 where the two distances cancel.
    Regret notion
    Deterministic cumulative loss difference on the same generated trajectory.
    Guarantee
    Cumulative comparator regret retains the negative terminal squared distance, with no bounded-domain premise.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Cumulative comparator regret retains the negative terminal squared distance, with no bounded-domain premise.\[R_T(u)\le\frac{\|x_1-u\|^2}{2\eta}+\frac\eta2\sum_{t=1}^T\|g_t\|^2-\frac{\|x_{T+1}-u\|^2}{2\eta}\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Exact shared declaration: BanditRL.OnlineGradientDescentSource.theorem_2_13_fixed. Sum the generated trajectory one-step inequality and telescope; divide by the positive fixed step. The proved source_to_feasible adapter supplies the source arbitrary-open regularity on the same old projected recurrence.

    Equation (2.1): horizon tuning

    Original at printed pp. 12-15 ↗
    Compiled local source-repair candidateCompiled local OGD source-repair candidate, with distinct source/body review. Full harness, actual graph, shared Book registry, final reader/site and PR gates remain separate. This is not all Chapter2 or the whole book, and is not merged or live.

    A priori diameter and gradient bounds yield the source square-root regret rate.

    Model
    Finite-dimensional real Euclidean projected OGD; Lean additionally supports real Hilbert spaces.
    Assumptions
    Nonempty closed convex V, feasible initial point; T,D,G>0; pairwise distances<=D and gradient norms<=G on this actual tuned trajectory. Convexity on V and a supplied ambient real extension differentiable on an arbitrary open U containing V. U need not be convex. source_to_feasible proves the conversion to FeasibleRegularLoss: convexity on V and ambient derivatives at every feasible point. On a thin V the supplied extension fixes the gradient; extension independence is not claimed.
    Algorithm parameters
    One horizon-prescribed eta=D/(G sqrtT) before the conclusion for all feasible comparators. No zero-parameter or anytime claim.
    Regret notion
    Deterministic cumulative loss difference on the same generated trajectory.
    Guarantee
    A priori diameter and gradient bounds yield the source square-root regret rate.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: A priori diameter and gradient bounds yield the source square-root regret rate.\[\eta=\frac{D}{G\sqrt T},\qquad R_T(u)\le DG\sqrt T\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Exact shared declaration: BanditRL.OnlineGradientDescentSource.equation_2_1. Bound initial distance and gradient energy, then simplify the positive horizon-tuned step. The proved source_to_feasible adapter supplies the source arbitrary-open regularity on the same old projected recurrence.

    The mathematical content is restated in this site's notation; wording is ours. See printed pp. 12-15 in the linked source for the original statement and full assumptions.

    Source routeTheorem 2.13: positive nonincreasing stepsCompiled local source-repair candidateprinted pp. 13-14＋
    Source theorem · faithful restatement

    Theorem 2.13: positive nonincreasing steps

    Original at printed pp. 13-14 ↗
    Compiled local source-repair candidateCompiled local OGD source-repair candidate, with distinct source/body review. Full harness, actual graph, shared Book registry, final reader/site and PR gates remain separate. This is not all Chapter2 or the whole book, and is not merged or live.

    The same generated trajectory determines all gradients and the terminal residual.

    Model
    Projected OGD in finite-dimensional real Euclidean space; the Lean interface supports real Hilbert spaces.
    Assumptions
    Nonempty closed convex bounded V; feasible initial point and comparator; T>=1; positive adjacent nonincreasing prescribed steps. Convexity on V and a supplied ambient real extension differentiable on an arbitrary open U containing V. U need not be convex. source_to_feasible proves the conversion to FeasibleRegularLoss: convexity on V and ambient derivatives at every feasible point. On a thin V the supplied extension fixes the gradient; extension independence is not claimed.
    Algorithm parameters
    Exact finite Metric.diam V. Both distance denominators use the last played step eta(T-1); D=0 is allowed.
    Regret notion
    Deterministic cumulative comparator loss difference for iterateVariable; source eta_T is Lean eta(T-1).
    Guarantee
    D squared over twice the final step, plus weighted gradient energy, minus the terminal squared distance over twice the final step.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Decreasing learning rates give a finite-diameter regret bound with the negative terminal squared distance retained.\[R_T(u)\le\frac{D^2}{2\eta_T}+\sum_{t=1}^T\frac{\eta_t}{2}\|g_t\|^2-\frac{\|x_{T+1}-u\|^2}{2\eta_T}\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Exact shared declaration: BanditRL.OnlineGradientDescentSource.theorem_2_13_variable. Sum actual variable_one_step inequalities through weighted_potential_sum; boundedness supplies the finite Metric.diam interface. The proved source_to_feasible adapter supplies the source arbitrary-open regularity on the same old projected recurrence.

    The mathematical content is restated in this site's notation; wording is ours. See printed pp. 13-14 in the linked source for the original statement and full assumptions.

    Theorem2.13 proof: exact weighted-distance equality

    Original at printed14 / PDF26 ↗
    Locally compiled and source-reviewed exact identityLocally compiled and independently source/BODY-reviewed exact identity with explicit algebraic generalization. Combined package gates remain pending. Chapter2 remains partial, accepted=false and complete proof denominator=null; all eight forward containers and six future claims remain open. Whole Chapters1-16 Goal ACTIVE; unmerged and unpublished.

    Changes of reciprocal step sizes leave an interior correction when squared-distance differences telescope. The exact initial and terminal potentials remain.

    Model
    Deterministic finite trajectory; Euclidean source, normed additive commutative group generalization.
    Assumptions
    T>=1; every played eta positive. No monotonicity needed for this equality.
    Algorithm parameters
    Source1..T maps to Lean0..T-1; terminal x_(T+1) maps to x(T).
    Regret notion
    No regret bound: exact weighted-distance identity only.
    Guarantee
    Full equation retains all endpoints and interior terms.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Initial potential minus terminal potential plus interior changes of reciprocal steps, with factor1/2.\[\begin{aligned}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\|x_t-u\|_2^2-\|x_{t+1}-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_t}&=\frac{\|x_1-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_1}-\frac{\|x_{T+1}-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_T}\\&\quad+\frac12\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\frac1{\eta_{t+1}}-\frac1{\eta_t}\right)\|x_{t+1}-u\|_2^2.\end{aligned}\]Swipe to read the full formula →

    BanditRLlib relationship. Unnumbered second display equality in the proof of Theorem2.13. Source Euclidean squared distances specialize an explicitly more general NormedAddCommGroup identity. The underlying real-sequence lemma allows signed/zero potentials and weights, no monotonicity and no terminal bound. The distance theorem retains T>0 and eta_t>0 at every played index. Lean index0 is source round1, x(T) is source x_(T+1), eta(T-1) is source eta_T. No loss regularity, feedback legality, feasible-set diameter or algorithm recursion is asserted by this algebraic equality. No missing-half source correction is proposed. Locally compiled and independently source/BODY-reviewed exact identity with explicit algebraic generalization. Combined package gates remain pending. Chapter2 remains partial, accepted=false and complete proof denominator=null; all eight forward containers and six future claims remain open. Whole Chapters1-16 Goal ACTIVE; unmerged and unpublished.

    The mathematical content is restated in this site's notation; wording is ours. See printed14 / PDF26 in the linked source for the original statement and full assumptions.

    Chapter2 prescient observation: affine Euclidean foundation only

    Original at printed14,265–266 / PDF26,277–278 ↗
    Affine prescient foundation compiled locally; general source remains openThis is an affine-loss quadratic Euclidean prescient foundation, explicitly extended to complete real Hilbert spaces; seven declarations form one producer chain, not seven printed results. The full general convex/subdifferentiable/Bregman/time-varying Algorithm15.8/Theorem15.30 and the full Chapter2 prescient source container remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the source constant-step proof left as an exercise. All eight Chapter2 mathematical forward containers remain open. Chapter2 and whole Chapters1–16 Goal are incomplete; no main/live, merge or deployment claim. This branch is stacked on exact unmerged PR204 head b1486aa11451556858fda44eabf356ba26ab01b8.

    Receive the current affine loss l_t(x)=inner(g_t,x)+b_t BEFORE playing p_t. State z0=x0, z_(t+1)=projectionV(z_t-eta*g_t), p_t=z_(t+1). Lean t=0 is source round1; source initial x0 is retained. This information order differs from Algorithm2.1, which plays before receiving current loss. Arbitrary future vectors cannot affect p_t; the current vector can. The real recursion is classical mathematical projection, not a numerical implementation or sampler. V is nonempty, closed and convex. Every feasible comparator u and every natural horizon T, including0. The initial center x0 is arbitrary in the ambient space and may lie outsideV. For minimization and regret inequalities eta>0 is fixed; feasibility/prefix/offset algebra need no positive eta. No bounded-domain or gradient-bound premise. Source X=E makes x0 interior automatic; finite Euclidean source instance extends here to complete real Hilbert E. Set p=projectionV(x-eta*g). The attained projection and variational characterization give inner(x-eta*g-p,u-p)<=0. Rearrange to eta inner(g,p-u)<=inner(x-p,p-u). Expand the three-point squared-distance identity and divide by positive2eta. This constructs the inequality from the actual projection, without a supplied regret certificate. Multiply the desired inequality by positive2eta. Induct onT and apply the actual one-step producer to stateT and currentgT. The two adjacent comparator-distance squares cancel exactly, while each actual movement square is subtracted. Divide back by2eta. The baseT=0 has empty regret and empty movement sum, with terminalstate0=x0. Apply the sharp same-run cumulative bound and remove only the nonnegative terminal-distance quotient. The negative movement energy remains. On constrainedV, this is not generally negative eta/2 times ordinary-gradient energy: projection can clip the step; the canary proves an explicit failure of that naive substitution. This is not an author erratum or a claim that the qualitative source observation is false.

    Model
    Receive the current affine loss l_t(x)=inner(g_t,x)+b_t BEFORE playing p_t. State z0=x0, z_(t+1)=projectionV(z_t-eta*g_t), p_t=z_(t+1). Lean t=0 is source round1; source initial x0 is retained. This information order differs from Algorithm2.1, which plays before receiving current loss. Arbitrary future vectors cannot affect p_t; the current vector can. The real recursion is classical mathematical projection, not a numerical implementation or sampler.
    Assumptions
    V is nonempty, closed and convex. Every feasible comparator u and every natural horizon T, including0. The initial center x0 is arbitrary in the ambient space and may lie outsideV. For minimization and regret inequalities eta>0 is fixed; feasibility/prefix/offset algebra need no positive eta. No bounded-domain or gradient-bound premise. Source X=E makes x0 interior automatic; finite Euclidean source instance extends here to complete real Hilbert E.
    Algorithm parameters
    Arbitrary fixed eta>0, entire vector/offset streams as mathematical input, but prediction depends only on current-inclusive finite prefix. Same chosen recursive path for every feasible fixed comparator and everyT.
    Regret notion
    Finite signed deterministic fixed-comparator cumulative affine loss at the post-update played points. No expectation, randomization or standard strict-past algorithm guarantee.
    Guarantee
    Actual proximal minimizing projection, feasibility, current-inclusive prefix, offset identity and same-run sharp cumulative bound. This is an affine-loss quadratic Euclidean prescient foundation, explicitly extended to complete real Hilbert spaces; seven declarations form one producer chain, not seven printed results.     Chapter2 prescient dependency: real nonsmooth minimizer comparison

    Original at printed14,11,265–266 / PDF26,23,277–278 ↗
    Real comparison and concrete instances compiled; general source openOne reusable real-valued convex minimizer comparison, explicitly generalized to arbitrary real normed spaces. This is a dependency of the Chapter2 prescient forward observation, not full Algorithm15.8/Theorem15.30 or a new printed theorem. The actual supplied minimizer is a premise of this helper, not an existence/uniqueness or causal-update conclusion. General Bregman/extended-real finite-domain/subgradient bridges, attained current-loss recursion/interior validity and same-run fixed/variable-step source bounds remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the main-text constant-step proof left as an exercise.     E is a real normed vector space; V is a subset, p belongs toV, and f:E->R is convex onV (including convexity ofV). The supplied p actually minimizes f+h overV. Only h has a specified ambient continuous Frechet derivative atp. No derivative or continuity of f, convexity of h, completeness/inner product/finite dimension, or closed/open/bounded/coercive V is assumed. EmptyV is ruled out by p membership; boundary/singleton/lower-dimensional sets and u=p are allowed. Every comparator u belongs to the sameV. Fix u inV and put q(a)=p+a(u-p),0<=a<=1. Feasibility follows from convexity ofV. Actual minimality and convexity of f give f(p)+h(p)<=f(q(a))+h(q(a))<=(1-a)f(p)+a f(u)+h(q(a)). Hence H(a)=a(f(u)-f(p))+h(q(a))-h(p)>=0=H(0). Differentiate only h along the affine segment: Hprime(0)=f(u)-f(p)+Dh(p)[u-p]. At a minimum on[0,1], the positive tangent direction1 gives Hprime(0)>=0. Rearranging yields f(p)-f(u)<=Dh(p)[u-p]. This proves the loss comparison from an actual minimum, without a supplied regret/stability bound or differentiability of the loss.

    Chapter2 prescient dependency: canonical Bregman algebra and real proximal step

    Original at printed14,63–64,265–266 / PDF26,75–76,277–278 ↗
    Bregman helpers compiled; full source remains openA necessary Bregman foundation for the Chapter2 prescient forward dependency, not complete Algorithm15.8/Theorem15.30 or acceptance of Chapter6/15. Source X/interior and local-extension validity, finite-domain extended-real loss/subgradient/convexity/minimum bridges, attained causal current-loss recursion/interior invariants, and sharp fixed/variable same-run telescopes remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the main-text fixed-step exercise.     One canonical total real function Dψ(a,b)=ψ(a)-ψ(b)-Dψ(b)[a-b], using the actual Frechet derivative fderiv of ψ. The second argument is the base. At a nondifferentiable base fderiv defaults to zero; this total algebraic formula alone is not a source differentiability, positivity, metric or separation theorem. The core uses any real normed vector space; only the gradient conversion needs a complete real inner-product space. The source is finite Euclidean, ψ:X->R strictly convex/differentiable on intX; source-domain and ambient-extension locality are not silently identified. The supplied p belongs to V and actually minimizes f(z)+ηinverse Dψ(z,x) there; f is real convex on the same V, η>0, and ψ is ambient differentiable at BOTH x and p. x may be outside V. No loss derivative, ψ convexity/strictness, closedness, coercivity or existence theorem is required by this real helper. It concludes the comparison for every u in V with both negative residuals retained. Nonnegative residual values require separate convexity hypotheses; they cannot be discarded from this theorem alone. Differentiate only h(z)=ηinverse Dψ(z,x) at the selected minimum p. Its derivative is ηinverse(Dψ(p)-Dψ(x)). The accepted real nonsmooth convex-minimizer comparison gives f(p)-f(u)<=ηinverse(Dψ(p)-Dψ(x))[u-p]. Multiply by positive η and use the three-point identity with (p,x,u), obtaining Dψ(u,x)-Dψ(u,p)-Dψ(p,x). The previous-base derivative hypothesis is retained for source validity even though the fixed dual map is already linear in this proof. This is an actual-minimum comparison, not an assumed regret/stability bound or a sequence construction. Two compiled public Test families prove actual strict convexity, polynomial derivatives and minimizing conditions: ψ(z)=z^4/4+z^2/2 with nonsmooth abs loss, center1/2 and minimizer p=0 (objective minimum value11/64); and ψ(z)=z^2/2 on[0,1] with center-1 outsideV and boundary minimizer p=0 (objective minimum value1/2). The ordered nonquadratic divergences are9/64 and11/64. Each universal comparison and separately selected final numerical proof branch retains actual public proximal_one_step VALUE; final bounds -1/2<=-5/16 and -1/2<=1/2 use equality transport of that comparison. Divergence formulas also use actual public gradient conversion. Nonnegative has a full generic public VALUE witness, with no claimed concrete Test VALUE use. These are illustrative instances, not new printed results, an existence theorem or a causal algorithm.

    Chapter 2 prescient dependency: extended losses on a finite feasible domain

    Extended-loss bridges compiled; full source remains openThese three derived bridges lower a required Chapter 2 prescient forward dependency.     The same EReal loss f, convex feasible set V and canonical ordered divergence Bψ(a;b)=ψ(a)−ψ(b)−Dψ(b)[a−b] are used throughout. Properness excludes negative infinity everywhere and supplies one finite point. At every feasible z, a source subgradient supports f at every ambient y. This global support condition implies that f is finite at every point of V; outside V, positive infinity is allowed. The first bridge uses an arbitrary real inner-product space. The other two explicitly retain completeness from the reused minimum-order API. Finite Euclidean source spaces are covered; minimal assumptions are not claimed. The result is convexity of z ↦ (f z).toReal restricted to V. Taking toReal at infinity gives zero, so the total real function need not be globally convex. The proof selects a global support at the feasible convex combination, compares the two feasible endpoints, and cancels the weighted inner products after feasible finiteness has been established. For a supplied p in V and finite f on V, the actual EReal objective f(z)+η⁻¹ Bψ(z;x) has a minimum at p exactly when its real finite-part objective does. This order equivalence permits every real η, including zero and negative values, with Lean’s total inverse. It needs no convexity, derivative, positive step size or attainment premise beyond the supplied minimum in each direction. The center x may be outside V and the loss domain. The proof reuses the shared minOn_finitePart_iff on the actual penalized objective. For the one-step comparison, η is positive, V is convex, f is proper and has global supports at every feasible point, p belongs to V and actually minimizes the EReal penalized objective there. The generator ψ has actual ambient derivatives at both x and p. Every u in the same V satisfies η((f p).toReal−(f u).toReal) ≤ Bψ(u;x)−Bψ(u;p)−Bψ(p;x). Both negative residuals and their target/base order are retained. No infinity subtraction, loss derivative, convexity of ψ, closedness of V, bounded domain or existence of minimizers is asserted. Dropping residuals requires separate nonnegativity hypotheses.

    Chapter 2 prescient dependency: actual partial updates and interior-base locality

    Original at printed 14,16-17,63-64,265-266 / PDF 26,28-29,75-76,277-278 ↗
    Partial causal update foundations compiled; full source remains openThis package adds eight derived proofs and two exact partial definitions for a required Chapter 2 prescient forward dependency. It does not complete Algorithm 15.8 or Theorem 15.30, or accept Chapters 6 or 15. Local ambient-extension equality and the actual current-only partial recursion are now proved; transport of the source X/interior regularity into a valid generated run, its source interior conditions, and sharp fixed/variable same-run cumulative bounds remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the main-text fixed-step exercise.     Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. The whole current loss is received before the played prediction. Lean state0 is source x0; Lean loss t is source round t+1 and played prediction is state(t+1). This is prescient feedback, not ordinary OGD prediction before the current loss. The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. Generic advance and recursion specs allow empty V, arbitrary real eta including zero/negative with total inverse, and improper/infinite objectives. Returning some means a genuinely feasible minimum, even an infinite minimum in those generic cases. Returning none means no feasible attained minimum, not timeout, numerical failure or nonuniqueness. The initial center may lie outside V and the loss domain. Source performance uses positive eta and its stated loss/regularity hypotheses. For the signed one-step result, both consecutive states must actually return some x and some p on the SAME recursion. V is convex, current eta is positive, the EReal loss is proper and has a global supporting subgradient at every feasible point. Each support compares all ambient y, not just feasible y; these premises establish finite compared loss values. Psi has actual ambient derivatives at BOTH x and p. Every u in V satisfies eta_t((loss_t p).toReal-(loss_t u).toReal) <= B_psi(u;x)-B_psi(u;p)-B_psi(p;x). The actual successor specification supplies the minimum and identifies its predecessor with x; the accepted extended-loss producer then applies. Both negative ordered residuals remain. No loss derivative, bounded domain, psi convexity or universal attainment is inferred. No infinity subtraction is used and residuals are not dropped without a separate nonnegativity argument.

No loss derivative, bounded domain, psi convexity or universal attainment is inferred. No infinity subtraction is used and residuals are not dropped without a separate nonnegativity argument.
    Algorithm parameters
    State0=x0; loss t/eta t consumed for state(t+1). Positive current eta only for performance; arbitrary eta for generic specs.
    Regret notion
    Same-produced-transition finite-part loss difference with both signed residuals, plus two finite concrete run instances. Full cumulative source endpoints remain open.
    Guarantee
    The whole current loss is received before the played prediction. Lean state0 is source x0; Lean loss t is source round t+1 and played prediction is state(t+1). This is prescient feedback, not ordinary OGD prediction before the current loss. The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. For the signed one-step result, both consecutive states must actually return some x and some p on the SAME recursion. V is convex, current eta is positive, the EReal loss is proper and has a global supporting subgradient at every feasible point. Each support compares all ambient y, not just feasible y; these premises establish finite compared loss values. Psi has actual ambient derivatives at BOTH x and p. Every u in V satisfies eta_t((loss_t p).toReal-(loss_t u).toReal) <= B_psi(u;x)-B_psi(u;p)-B_psi(p;x). The actual successor specification supplies the minimum and identifies its predecessor with x; the accepted extended-loss producer then applies. Both negative ordered residuals remain. No loss derivative, bounded domain, psi convexity or universal attainment is inferred. No infinity subtraction is used and residuals are not dropped without a separate nonnegativity argument. Four complete public Test conjunctions audit the boundary. Restricted absolute then different restricted affine -5z/8 on [-1,1], with psi=z^4/4+z^2/2, produce actual selected states 1/2 -> 0 -> 1/2; both losses are top outside the interval and abs is nonsmooth at zero. Genuine minima and uniqueness determine the chosen states; conditional local-attainment completion, actual successor extraction, whole-function prefix invariance and both actual one-step transitions are exercised. Ordered movements are 11/64 and 9/64; the two-round comparison gives -1/2 <= -5/16 at u=1/2. A restricted linear loss on [0,1] with quadratic psi starts at -1 outside BOTH V and the loss domain, selects boundary0 and gives movement1/2 and -1/2 <= 1/2. Both numerical tails retain the new actual-transition comparison by Eq.mp. For V=(-infinity,0], f(z)=z and psi=exp, source closed real-threshold sublevels, strict convexity and differentiability hold, yet the objective exp(z)-1 has no feasible minimum: p-1 always improves it. Advance is none and every positive state is none. This is a definedness audit, not a complete generated source run or author erratum. Finally z^2+z and z^2+abs(z) agree on X=[0,infinity); at interior base1/target2 the new locality theorem gives equal value1. At boundarybase0 their TOTAL-fderiv formulas give4 and6, and the second representative is proved nondifferentiable there. Those boundary values are diagnostics, not source-valid divergences. These four illustrative conjunctions are not additional printed results or canonical Book nodes.     For every nonnegative initial offset c, natural finite horizon T, real increment stream b with b_t>=0 for t<T, and real function f continuous, nonincreasing and nonnegative on [0,infinity), define s_0=c and s_(t+1)=s_t+b_t. Then sum(t<T)b_t*f(s_(t+1)) is at most the integral of f from c to s_T. All zero cases are included.

    Model
    Deterministic finite-increment right-endpoint integration comparison.
    Assumptions
    Nonnegative initial offset and played increments; f continuous, nonincreasing and nonnegative on the nonnegative half-line.
    Algorithm parameters
    Source increments1..T; Lean increments0..T-1; initial offset kept separate. Empty horizon and zero increments allowed.
    Regret notion
    No regret/algorithm guarantee is asserted by this lemma.
    Guarantee
    The two full public canaries include a nonconstant f(x)=max(1-x,0), offset0 and increments[1/2,0,1/2], with exact weighted sum1/4, exact integral1/2 and strict gap; they also separately test horizon0 and a nonempty allzero increment prefix at offset2 with f(2)=1. All three inequality conjuncts retain actual public-lemma VALUE calls. Direct constant presence is not proof necessity or a complete transitive graph. The adjacent teaching note gives the displayed-formula proof, exact folded Lean and actual dependencies.

    The actual alpha=sqrt(2)/2 adaptive run satisfies R_T(u)<=D*sqrt(2*S_T). Its numerical bound equals sqrt(2) times the infimum of the fixed-energy positive-step upper-bound family. The regret inequality preserves the source regimes; the infimum display explicitly repairs unattained one-zero minima.

    Model
    Deterministic actual causal full-information projected adaptive subgradient run.
    Assumptions
    Feasible initial point; finite-dimensional real inner-product space; nonempty closed convex domain of diameter at most D>=0; finite horizon; proper losses subdifferentiable on the domain; chosen supports legal at actual played points. Source specializations additionally retain convexity. General alpha endpoint requires alpha>0 and is support-sufficient without an extra convexity premise.
    Algorithm parameters
    Source1..T to Lean0..T-1; inclusive energy in the learning rate; terminal outputT.
    Regret notion
    The actual alpha=sqrt(2)/2 adaptive run satisfies R_T(u)<=D*sqrt(2*S_T). Its numerical bound equals sqrt(2) times the infimum of the fixed-energy positive-step upper-bound family. The regret inequality preserves the source regimes; the infimum display explicitly repairs unattained one-zero minima.
    Guarantee
    The exact Lean statement is folded in the adjacent teaching highlight; its natural-language/formula proof is in the teaching notes, and the complete Lean proof BODY is available through the source link. Separate source-display repair, not a change to the original PDF or regret inequality: for B(eta)=D^2/(2eta)+eta*S/2 on eta>0, the infimum is D*sqrt(S) for every D,S>=0. It is attained exactly when both D,S are positive or both are zero. When both are positive the unique minimizer is D/sqrt(S); when both are zero every positive eta minimizes; when exactly one vanishes the infimum0 is unattained. Theorem4.14 displayed minimum must therefore be read as infimum in the one-zero regimes. S is the energy realized by the single adaptive run, frozen during this scalar comparison; no optimal rerun or clairvoyant learner is asserted.
    Source mathematical statement. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: The actual alpha=sqrt(2)/2 adaptive run satisfies R_T(u)<=D*sqrt(2*S_T).     Why changing step sizes leaves an interior correction

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineWeightedPotential.distance_potential_sum_eq

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Changes of reciprocal step sizes leave an interior correction when squared-distance differences telescope. The exact initial and terminal potentials remain. For every positive horizon and positive played step sizes, the displayed equality holds exactly.

    Mathematical reading. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Changes of reciprocal step sizes leave an interior correction when squared-distance differences telescope. The exact initial and terminal potentials remain. For every positive horizon and positive played step sizes, the displayed equality holds exactly.\[\begin{aligned}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\|x_t-u\|_2^2-\|x_{t+1}-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_t}&=\frac{\|x_1-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_1}-\frac{\|x_{T+1}-u\|_2^2}{2\eta_T}\\&\quad+\frac12\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\frac1{\eta_{t+1}}-\frac1{\eta_t}\right)\|x_{t+1}-u\|_2^2.\end{aligned}\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    Intuition
    With constant weights, adjacent potentials cancel completely; changing weights leaves their successive differences.
    Why it is needed
    Expose the source algebra without weakening it to a diameter inequality or dropping the terminal residual.
    Place in the proof
    Orabona v10, printed14/PDF26, Theorem2.13 proof second display equality. Locally compiled and independently source/BODY-reviewed exact identity with explicit algebraic generalization. Combined package gates remain pending. Chapter2 remains partial, accepted=false and complete proof denominator=null; all eight forward containers and six future claims remain open. Whole Chapters1-16 Goal ACTIVE; unmerged and unpublished.
    Proof and Lean reading notes
    Proof idea
    Write a_t for the squared distance and w_t for 1/(2 eta_t). Expand each difference a_t w_t-a_(t+1)w_t. The initial a_1w_1 occurs once positively and the terminal a_(T+1)w_T once negatively. Each interior a_(t+1) occurs with coefficient w_(t+1)-w_t. Collect these coefficients and substitute reciprocal steps, retaining the factor1/2. The printed full equality includes the same gradient-energy sum sum eta_t||g_t||^2/2 on each side; add that common sum back to this extracted identity to recover the full display, or cancel it to isolate this identity. T=1 has no interior sum.
    Lean reading notes
    Source Euclidean squared distances specialize an explicitly more general NormedAddCommGroup identity. The underlying real-sequence lemma allows signed/zero potentials and weights, no monotonicity and no terminal bound. The distance theorem retains T>0 and eta_t>0 at every played index. Lean index0 is source round1, x(T) is source x_(T+1), eta(T-1) is source eta_T. No loss regularity, feedback legality, feasible-set diameter or algorithm recursion is asserted by this algebraic equality. No missing-half source correction is proposed. The distance proof actually reuses BanditRL.OnlineWeightedPotential.weighted_difference_sum_eq. The scalar companion VALUE contains Finset.sum_range_by_parts and Finset.sum_range_sub'. These are selected actual compiled VALUE occurrences, not necessity claims or a complete transitive graph. Exact folded statements and full proof source links expose both declarations through the shared catalog. This information order differs from Algorithm2.1, which plays before receiving current loss. Arbitrary future vectors cannot affect p_t; the current vector can.     Nonsmooth proximal foundation: compare the convex loss at an actual minimizer

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineProximal.convex_minimizer_comparison

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. A convex loss need not be differentiable to obtain this comparison. Supply an actual minimum of loss plus a differentiable perturbation, then compare the loss with every feasible competitor. E is a real normed vector space; V is a subset, p belongs toV, and f:E->R is convex onV (including convexity ofV). The supplied p actually minimizes f+h overV. Only h has a specified ambient continuous Frechet derivative atp. No derivative or continuity of f, convexity of h, completeness/inner product/finite dimension, or closed/open/bounded/coercive V is assumed. EmptyV is ruled out by p membership; boundary/singleton/lower-dimensional sets and u=p are allowed. Every comparator u belongs to the sameV.

    Mathematical reading. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: A convex loss need not be differentiable to obtain this comparison. Supply an actual minimum of loss plus a differentiable perturbation, then compare the loss with every feasible competitor. E is a real normed vector space; V is a subset, p belongs toV, and f:E->R is convex onV (including convexity ofV). The supplied p actually minimizes f+h overV. Only h has a specified ambient continuous Frechet derivative atp. No derivative or continuity of f, convexity of h, completeness/inner product/finite dimension, or closed/open/bounded/coercive V is assumed. EmptyV is ruled out by p membership; boundary/singleton/lower-dimensional sets and u=p are allowed. Every comparator u belongs to the sameV.\[\begin{aligned}p&\in V,\quad p\in\arg\min_{z\in V}(f+h)(z),\\f(p)-f(u)&\le Dh(p)[u-p]\quad(u\in V),\\q(a)&=p+a(u-p),\\H(a)&=a(f(u)-f(p))+h(q(a))-h(p),\\0&\le H\prime(0)=f(u)-f(p)+Dh(p)[u-p].\end{aligned}\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    Intuition
    Move a small positive distance toward a feasible comparator. Convexity controls the loss by its endpoint values; only the perturbation is differentiated.
    Why it is needed
    Lower the required general prescient proof frontier without imposing smoothness on the loss.
    Place in the proof
    Orabona, arXiv:1912.13213v10,2026-06-21; Chapter2 printed14/PDF26 prescient forward observation -> Section15.5.1, Algorithm15.8/Theorem15.30 printed265–266/PDF277–278. Derived loss-comparison dependency, not the complete source theorem. Theorem2.8 printed11/PDF23 explicitly assumes a differentiable objective and is not applied to a nonsmooth f+h here. PDF SHA cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17.
    Proof and Lean reading notes
    Proof idea
    Fix u inV and put q(a)=p+a(u-p),0<=a<=1. Feasibility follows from convexity ofV. Actual minimality and convexity of f give f(p)+h(p)<=f(q(a))+h(q(a))<=(1-a)f(p)+a f(u)+h(q(a)). Hence H(a)=a(f(u)-f(p))+h(q(a))-h(p)>=0=H(0). Differentiate only h along the affine segment: Hprime(0)=f(u)-f(p)+Dh(p)[u-p]. At a minimum on[0,1], the positive tangent direction1 gives Hprime(0)>=0. Rearranging yields f(p)-f(u)<=Dh(p)[u-p]. This proves the loss comparison from an actual minimum, without a supplied regret/stability bound or differentiability of the loss.
    Lean reading notes
    E is a real normed vector space; V is a subset, p belongs toV, and f:E->R is convex onV (including convexity ofV). The supplied p actually minimizes f+h overV. Only h has a specified ambient continuous Frechet derivative atp. No derivative or continuity of f, convexity of h, completeness/inner product/finite dimension, or closed/open/bounded/coercive V is assumed. EmptyV is ruled out by p membership; boundary/singleton/lower-dimensional sets and u=p are allowed. Every comparator u belongs to the sameV. Actual pinned Mathlib facts: IsLocalMinOn.hasFDerivWithinAt_nonneg, sub_mem_posTangentConeAt_of_segment_subset, Convex.segment_subset, HasFDerivAt.comp_hasDerivAt_of_eq, scalar derivative composition/addition, and continuous-linear evaluation. No previous ABRL theorem is a direct VALUE parent of this proof; the source mapping and planned Bregman consumer are overlays, not formal implication edges. Three actual Test families prove their minimizing conditions and derivative data: f(z)=abs(z),h(z)=(z-1/2)^2/2,p=0 with a genuinely nonsmooth loss; f(z)=z,h(z)=-z/2 on[0,1],p=0, which is not a whole-line minimum; and f(z)=z^2,h(z)=-z^2/2+z,p=-1 with a nonconvex regularizer.     Canonical ordered Bregman formula

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineBregman.divergence

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. One canonical total real function Dψ(a,b)=ψ(a)-ψ(b)-Dψ(b)[a-b], using the actual Frechet derivative fderiv of ψ. The second argument is the base. At a nondifferentiable base fderiv defaults to zero; this total algebraic formula alone is not a source differentiability, positivity, metric or separation theorem.     Nonsmooth proximal comparison retains both residuals

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineBregman.proximal_one_step

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. One canonical total real function Dψ(a,b)=ψ(a)-ψ(b)-Dψ(b)[a-b], using the actual Frechet derivative fderiv of ψ. The second argument is the base. At a nondifferentiable base fderiv defaults to zero; this total algebraic formula alone is not a source differentiability, positivity, metric or separation theorem. The source is finite Euclidean, ψ:X->R strictly convex/differentiable on intX; source-domain and ambient-extension locality are not silently identified. The supplied p belongs to V and actually minimizes f(z)+ηinverse Dψ(z,x) there; f is real convex on the same V, η>0, and ψ is ambient differentiable at BOTH x and p. x may be outside V. No loss derivative, ψ convexity/strictness, closedness, coercivity or existence theorem is required by this real helper. It concludes the comparison for every u in V with both negative residuals retained. Nonnegative residual values require separate convexity hypotheses; they cannot be discarded from this theorem alone.

    Proof and Lean reading notes
    Proof idea
    Differentiate only h(z)=ηinverse Dψ(z,x) at the selected minimum p. Its derivative is ηinverse(Dψ(p)-Dψ(x)). The accepted real nonsmooth convex-minimizer comparison gives f(p)-f(u)<=ηinverse(Dψ(p)-Dψ(x))[u-p]. Multiply by positive η and use the three-point identity with (p,x,u), obtaining Dψ(u,x)-Dψ(u,p)-Dψ(p,x). The previous-base derivative hypothesis is retained for source validity even though the fixed dual map is already linear in this proof. This is an actual-minimum comparison, not an assumed regret/stability bound or a sequence construction.

    Global supports give convexity on the finite feasible domain

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineBregman.finitePart_convex_of_subdifferentiable

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. The same EReal loss f, convex feasible set V and canonical ordered divergence Bψ(a;b)=ψ(a)−ψ(b)−Dψ(b)[a−b] are used throughout. Properness excludes negative infinity everywhere and supplies one finite point. At every feasible z, a source subgradient supports f at every ambient y. This global support condition implies that f is finite at every point of V; outside V, positive infinity is allowed. The first bridge uses an arbitrary real inner-product space. The other two explicitly retain completeness from the reused minimum-order API. Finite Euclidean source spaces are covered; minimal assumptions are not claimed. The result is convexity of z ↦ (f z).toReal restricted to V. Taking toReal at infinity gives zero, so the total real function need not be globally convex. The proof selects a global support at the feasible convex combination, compares the two feasible endpoints, and cancels the weighted inner products after feasible finiteness has been established.

    Compiled

    Lean reading notes
    Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. The whole current loss is received before the played prediction. Lean state0 is source x0; Lean loss t is source round t+1 and played prediction is state(t+1). This is prescient feedback, not ordinary OGD prediction before the current loss. The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. Generic advance and recursion specs allow empty V, arbitrary real eta including zero/negative with total inverse, and improper/infinite objectives. Returning some means a genuinely feasible minimum, even an infinite minimum in those generic cases. Returning none means no feasible attained minimum, not timeout, numerical failure or nonuniqueness. The initial center may lie outside V and the loss domain. Source performance uses positive eta and its stated loss/regularity hypotheses. Equality on X and an interior base imply neighborhood equality. The actual local fderiv congruence gives equal derivatives at b, and equality of values at a,b finishes the ordered formula. No differentiability or convexity premise is needed for this algebraic equality; it does not supply regularity, an extension, positivity or equality of selected trajectories. Four complete public Test conjunctions audit the boundary. Restricted absolute then different restricted affine -5z/8 on [-1,1], with psi=z^4/4+z^2/2, produce actual selected states 1/2 -> 0 -> 1/2; both losses are top outside the interval and abs is nonsmooth at zero. Genuine minima and uniqueness determine the chosen states; conditional local-attainment completion, actual successor extraction, whole-function prefix invariance and both actual one-step transitions are exercised. Ordered movements are 11/64 and 9/64; the two-round comparison gives -1/2 <= -5/16 at u=1/2. A restricted linear loss on [0,1] with quadratic psi starts at -1 outside BOTH V and the loss domain, selects boundary0 and gives movement1/2 and -1/2 <= 1/2. Both numerical tails retain the new actual-transition comparison by Eq.mp. For V=(-infinity,0], f(z)=z and psi=exp, source closed real-threshold sublevels, strict convexity and differentiability hold, yet the objective exp(z)-1 has no feasible minimum: p-1 always improves it. Advance is none and every positive state is none. This is a definedness audit, not a complete generated source run or author erratum. Finally z^2+z and z^2+abs(z) agree on X=[0,infinity); at interior base1/target2 the new locality theorem gives equal value1. At boundarybase0 their TOTAL-fderiv formulas give4 and6, and the second representative is proved nondifferentiable there. Those boundary values are diagnostics, not source-valid divergences.     Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.advance

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. This exact definition tests existence of p in V that minimizes the actual EReal penalized current objective, chooses that witness if one exists, and returns none otherwise. Membership is part of the existence guard. It takes only V, psi, current eta, current loss and old x; no desired regret premise. Infinite minima remain possible in the unrestricted generic definition.

The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. Returning some means a genuinely feasible minimum, even an infinite minimum in those generic cases. Returning none means no feasible attained minimum, not timeout, numerical failure or nonuniqueness.     Mathematics ↔ Lean

    Failure is exactly absence of a feasible attained minimum

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.advance_none_iff

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. Both directions use the exact same feasibility-and-minimum existential as the definition. When the existential holds the chosen some cannot be none; when it fails the definition is none. Empty sets are allowed. Nonuniqueness does not cause failure, and this equivalence is not an equivalence with unboundedness.

    Mathematical reading.     Mathematics ↔ Lean

    The actual state depends only on the consumed prefix

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.iterate_prefix

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. Two runs sharing V, psi and x0 have identical state t when steps and WHOLE current loss functions agree below t. This holds even for failed states and arbitrary real steps. The played point at t+1 uses loss t as well as earlier losses; the result does not convert prescient feedback into ordinary strict-past feedback.

    Mathematical reading.     Intuition
    Induct on t; the predecessor states agree by the shorter prefix, then the equal current eta/loss give identical bind calls.

    Proof and Lean reading notes
    Proof idea
    Induct on t; the predecessor states agree by the shorter prefix, then the equal current eta/loss give identical bind calls.
    Lean reading notes
    Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. The whole current loss is received before the played prediction. Lean state0 is source x0; Lean loss t is source round t+1 and played prediction is state(t+1). This is prescient feedback, not ordinary OGD prediction before the current loss. The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. Generic advance and recursion specs allow empty V, arbitrary real eta including zero/negative with total inverse, and improper/infinite objectives. Returning some means a genuinely feasible minimum, even an infinite minimum in those generic cases. Returning none means no feasible attained minimum, not timeout, numerical failure or nonuniqueness. The initial center may lie outside V and the loss domain. Source performance uses positive eta and its stated loss/regularity hypotheses. Two runs sharing V, psi and x0 have identical state t when steps and WHOLE current loss functions agree below t. This holds even for failed states and arbitrary real steps. The played point at t+1 uses loss t as well as earlier losses; the result does not convert prescient feedback into ordinary strict-past feedback. Four complete public Test conjunctions audit the boundary. Restricted absolute then different restricted affine -5z/8 on [-1,1], with psi=z^4/4+z^2/2, produce actual selected states 1/2 -> 0 -> 1/2; both losses are top outside the interval and abs is nonsmooth at zero. Genuine minima and uniqueness determine the chosen states; conditional local-attainment completion, actual successor extraction, whole-function prefix invariance and both actual one-step transitions are exercised. Ordered movements are 11/64 and 9/64; the two-round comparison gives -1/2 <= -5/16 at u=1/2. A restricted linear loss on [0,1] with quadratic psi starts at -1 outside BOTH V and the loss domain, selects boundary0 and gives movement1/2 and -1/2 <= 1/2. Both numerical tails retain the new actual-transition comparison by Eq.mp. For V=(-infinity,0], f(z)=z and psi=exp, source closed real-threshold sublevels, strict convexity and differentiability hold, yet the objective exp(z)-1 has no feasible minimum: p-1 always improves it. Advance is none and every positive state is none. This is a definedness audit, not a complete generated source run or author erratum. Finally z^2+z and z^2+abs(z) agree on X=[0,infinity); at interior base1/target2 the new locality theorem gives equal value1. At boundarybase0 their TOTAL-fderiv formulas give4 and6, and the second representative is proved nondifferentiable there. Those boundary values are diagnostics, not source-valid divergences. These four illustrative conjunctions are not additional printed results or canonical Book nodes. This package adds eight derived proofs and two exact partial definitions for a required Chapter 2 prescient forward dependency. It does not complete Algorithm 15.8 or Theorem 15.30, or accept Chapters 6 or 15. Local ambient-extension equality and the actual current-only partial recursion are now proved; transport of the source X/interior regularity into a valid generated run, its source interior conditions, and sharp fixed/variable same-run cumulative bounds remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the main-text fixed-step exercise. Earlier package boundary paragraphs describe their respective bounded scopes; the new locality and partial-state results are additive progress. All eight Chapter 2 forward containers stay open; Chapter 2 is partial, its complete proof denominator remains null, and the Chapters 1-16 Goal is ACTIVE.     A missing state remains missing at every later offset

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.iterate_no_recovery

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. Every natural offset k, including zero, preserves none. A premise at time0 is impossible because state0 is some x0. This describes the exact partial recursion and does not provide an efficient test of minimizer existence or a restart policy.

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.iterate_complete_of_step_attained

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. Assume for every reached some-state below T that its actual current EReal objective attains a feasible minimum. The same recursion then returns some at T. This is a proof premise about actual reached states, not an oracle read by the producer and not attainment deduced from closedness/strict convexity. T=0 needs no attainment and may return x0 outside V.

Each support compares all ambient y, not just feasible y; these premises establish finite compared loss values. Psi has actual ambient derivatives at BOTH x and p. No loss derivative, bounded domain, psi convexity or universal attainment is inferred. No infinity subtraction is used and residuals are not dropped without a separate nonnegativity argument.

    Lean reading notes
    Use the same canonical ordered divergence B_psi(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. The source divergence has domain X times interior X. An ambient total fderiv defaults to zero at nondifferentiable points, so its boundary values are not licensed as source derivatives. The locality and partial-state proofs use arbitrary real normed vector spaces; only the actual-transition loss bound retains the accepted parent complete real inner-product context. Finite Euclidean source spaces are included. The whole current loss is received before the played prediction. Lean state0 is source x0; Lean loss t is source round t+1 and played prediction is state(t+1). This is prescient feedback, not ordinary OGD prediction before the current loss. The producer consumes one current loss and step through Option.bind. It receives no horizon, comparator or future-loss oracle. Classical choice is noncomputable mathematical selection, not an executable optimizer or a measurable-selection theorem. Generic advance and recursion specs allow empty V, arbitrary real eta including zero/negative with total inverse, and improper/infinite objectives. Returning some means a genuinely feasible minimum, even an infinite minimum in those generic cases. Returning none means no feasible attained minimum, not timeout, numerical failure or nonuniqueness. The initial center may lie outside V and the loss domain. Source performance uses positive eta and its stated loss/regularity hypotheses. For the signed one-step result, both consecutive states must actually return some x and some p on the SAME recursion. V is convex, current eta is positive, the EReal loss is proper and has a global supporting subgradient at every feasible point. Each support compares all ambient y, not just feasible y; these premises establish finite compared loss values. Psi has actual ambient derivatives at BOTH x and p. Every u in V satisfies eta_t((loss_t p).toReal-(loss_t u).toReal) <= B_psi(u;x)-B_psi(u;p)-B_psi(p;x). The actual successor specification supplies the minimum and identifies its predecessor with x; the accepted extended-loss producer then applies. Both negative ordered residuals remain. No loss derivative, bounded domain, psi convexity or universal attainment is inferred. No infinity subtraction is used and residuals are not dropped without a separate nonnegativity argument. Four complete public Test conjunctions audit the boundary. Restricted absolute then different restricted affine -5z/8 on [-1,1], with psi=z^4/4+z^2/2, produce actual selected states 1/2 -> 0 -> 1/2; both losses are top outside the interval and abs is nonsmooth at zero. Genuine minima and uniqueness determine the chosen states; conditional local-attainment completion, actual successor extraction, whole-function prefix invariance and both actual one-step transitions are exercised. Ordered movements are 11/64 and 9/64; the two-round comparison gives -1/2 <= -5/16 at u=1/2. A restricted linear loss on [0,1] with quadratic psi starts at -1 outside BOTH V and the loss domain, selects boundary0 and gives movement1/2 and -1/2 <= 1/2. Both numerical tails retain the new actual-transition comparison by Eq.mp. For V=(-infinity,0], f(z)=z and psi=exp, source closed real-threshold sublevels, strict convexity and differentiability hold, yet the objective exp(z)-1 has no feasible minimum: p-1 always improves it. Advance is none and every positive state is none. This is a definedness audit, not a complete generated source run or author erratum. Finally z^2+z and z^2+abs(z) agree on X=[0,infinity); at interior base1/target2 the new locality theorem gives equal value1. At boundarybase0 their TOTAL-fderiv formulas give4 and6, and the second representative is proved nondifferentiable there. Those boundary values are diagnostics, not source-valid divergences. These four illustrative conjunctions are not additional printed results or canonical Book nodes. This package adds eight derived proofs and two exact partial definitions for a required Chapter 2 prescient forward dependency. It does not complete Algorithm 15.8 or Theorem 15.30, or accept Chapters 6 or 15. Local ambient-extension equality and the actual current-only partial recursion are now proved; transport of the source X/interior regularity into a valid generated run, its source interior conditions, and sharp fixed/variable same-run cumulative bounds remain REQUIRED/OPEN, including the main-text fixed-step exercise. Earlier package boundary paragraphs describe their respective bounded scopes; the new locality and partial-state results are additive progress.     Bound the weighted telescope and keep its final negative term

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlinePrescientBregman.iterate_variable_sharp

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Use the canonical ordered Bregman expression B(a;b)=psi(a)-psi(b)-Dpsi(b)[a-b], with target first and derivative base second. Source spaces are finite Euclidean and the generator is defined on X; the reusable Lean interfaces use a complete real inner-product space and an ambient representative, differentiable on interior X. Every actual state through T lies in interior X. This licenses the actual derivatives, not boundary values of a default total fderiv. V is convex; each played extended-real loss is SourceProper and has a global ambient supporting subgradient at every feasible point. These supports compare every ambient y and certify finite compared loss values; no infinity subtraction is used. Steps before T are strictly positive. The same canonical iterate returns some(x(t)) for every t<=T; this is a posthoc mathematical success hypothesis, not an input to the algorithm. The whole current loss is read before its paid successor prediction. Source loss_(t+1) is Lean loss t, source x0 is Lean x0, and source eta_T is Lean eta(T-1). No comparator, desired regret inequality, future oracle, executable optimizer, measurable selection or universal attainment is assumed as an algorithm. The shared iterate_divergence_sum interface allows every natural horizon T, including T=0, and any schedule with eta_t>0 for t<T; it assumes no monotonicity. The fixed-step interfaces allow T=0 with a positive constant step. Only iterate_variable_sharp and iterate_variable_regret require T>0 and eta_(t+1)<=eta_t for t+1<T; their cap or finite maximum concerns previous states 0,...,T-1, not x(T). No bounded domain or nonnegative arbitrary M is added to sharp interfaces. Printed corollaries additionally assume V subset X and strict convexity on X, and derive terminal B>=0 before dropping its negative term. Negative movement terms remain in both printed corollaries.

Drop only its negative term divided by the positive last played step; retain all weighted movements. This is a fixed-horizon deterministic statement, without an empty-maximum convention or future round eta(T).

    A right-endpoint sum is bounded by its integral

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveSummation.lemma_4_13

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), Lemma4.13, printed40/PDF52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For every nonnegative initial offset c, natural finite horizon T, real increment stream b with b_t>=0 for t<T, and real function f continuous, nonincreasing and nonnegative on [0,infinity), define s_0=c and s_(t+1)=s_t+b_t. Then sum(t<T)b_t*f(s_(t+1)) is at most the integral of f from c to s_T. All zero cases are included.

    Mathematical reading. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), Lemma4.13, printed40/PDF52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For every nonnegative initial offset c, natural finite horizon T, real increment stream b with b_t>=0 for t<T, and real function f continuous, nonincreasing and nonnegative on [0,infinity), define s_0=c and s_(t+1)=s_t+b_t. Then sum(t<T)b_t*f(s_(t+1)) is at most the integral of f from c to s_T. All zero cases are included.\[\begin{gathered}s_0=c,\quad s_{t+1}=s_t+b_t,\quad 0\le t<T,\\ \sum_{t=0}^{T-1}b_t f(s_{t+1})\le\int_c^{s_T}f(x)\,dx,\\ b_t f(s_{t+1})=\int_{s_t}^{s_{t+1}}f(s_{t+1})\,dx\le\int_{s_t}^{s_{t+1}}f(x)\,dx,\\ \sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\int_{s_t}^{s_{t+1}}f(x)\,dx=\int_c^{s_T}f(x)\,dx.\end{gathered}\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    Intuition
    Nonnegative increments order all cumulative endpoints inside the nonnegative half-line. Restrict the given continuity to each compact increment interval to obtain integrability. Antitonicity bounds the constant right-endpoint value by f(x) on that interval. Its integral is the actual increment times the right-endpoint value. Sum these comparisons and telescope the adjacent integrals; repeated endpoints and the empty horizon require no division or special positivity assumption.
    Why it is needed
    Supply a source-faithful reusable prerequisite for the adaptive learning-rate dependency of Chapter2.

    Proof and Lean reading notes
    Proof idea
    Nonnegative increments order all cumulative endpoints inside the nonnegative half-line. Restrict the given continuity to each compact increment interval to obtain integrability. Antitonicity bounds the constant right-endpoint value by f(x) on that interval. Its integral is the actual increment times the right-endpoint value. Sum these comparisons and telescope the adjacent integrals; repeated endpoints and the empty horizon require no division or special positivity assumption.
    Lean reading notes
    Lean b_t is source a_(t+1), while c is the separate source a0. The ambient real extension is constrained only on the nonnegative half-line, including relative continuity at zero; negative arguments are unused. The public type retains source f>=0 although this proof does not use that premise. No positivity, global regularity or integrability premise is added. Actual Mathlib VALUE dependencies: ContinuousOn.intervalIntegrable_of_Icc, intervalIntegral.integral_mono_on, intervalIntegral.integral_const, intervalIntegral.sum_integral_adjacent_intervals. No earlier ABRL theorem is a direct parent.
