    Adaptive energy sums telescope across zero prefixes

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveEnergy.source_energy_term_bound

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), unnumbered energy display after Lemma4.13 and before Eq.(4.4), printed40/PDF52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For every finite horizon T, normed additive commutative group E, feedback stream g from natural indices to E and nonnegative scalar D, the sum of squared feedback norms divided by the square root of their cumulative energy, multiplied by D/2, is at most D times the square root of total energy. An abstract nonnegative increment theorem and its squared-norm specialization supply the same bound.

    Mathematical reading. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), unnumbered energy display after Lemma4.13 and before Eq.(4.4), printed40/PDF52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For every finite horizon T, normed additive commutative group E, feedback stream g from natural indices to E and nonnegative scalar D, the sum of squared feedback norms divided by the square root of their cumulative energy, multiplied by D/2, is at most D times the square root of total energy. An abstract nonnegative increment theorem and its squared-norm specialization supply the same bound.\[\begin{gathered}S_{-1}=0,\quad S_t=\sum_{i=0}^{t}a_i,\quad a_i\ge0,\\ \frac{a_t}{\sqrt{S_t}}\le2(\sqrt{S_t}-\sqrt{S_{t-1}}),\\ \sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{a_t}{\sqrt{S_t}}\le2\sqrt{S_{T-1}},\\ a_t=\|g_t\|^2,\quad \frac D2\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{\|g_t\|^2}{\sqrt{S_t}}\le D\sqrt{S_{T-1}}.\end{gathered}\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    Intuition
    Write S_t for the cumulative nonnegative energy through the current increment. If S_t is positive, the shared square-root supporting line gives a_t/sqrt(S_t) <= 2(sqrt(S_t)-sqrt(S_(t-1))). If S_t=0, nonnegativity forces both the increment and preceding sum to be zero, giving the same inequality. Inductively add these inequalities: adjacent square roots telescope and the initial sum is zero. Substitute a_t=norm(g_t)^2, then multiply by the nonnegative scalar D/2 and cancel the numerical factor2.
    Why it is needed
    Supply the energy term needed by the required causal adaptive OSD proof.
    Place in the proof

    Causal adaptive OSD retains the terminal-distance residual

    Lean declarationBanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveOSD.regret_bound

    Compiled

    Plain-English statement. Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), printed13-14/PDF25-26 and printed39-40/PDF51-52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For the actual causal projected trajectory with eta_t=alpha*D/sqrt(S_(t+1)) and zero-feedback skipping, the regret is bounded by (1/(2alpha)+alpha)*D*sqrt(S_T) minus the terminal squared distance times sqrt(S_T)/(2alpha D). The reusable potential theorem separately permits signed real a_t bounded above by C only at played indices, nonnegative nondecreasing weights and T>0; no terminal bound or lower bound on a_t is added.

    Mathematical reading. Formula renderer unavailable; readable fallback: Orabona, Online Learning: A Modern Introduction Using Convex Optimization, arXiv:1912.13213v10 (2026-06-21), printed13-14/PDF25-26 and printed39-40/PDF51-52; SHA256 cef4edfa97a6e063e53e9c532717c50aa156e5bc782ea49f969b3385011a1b17. For the actual causal projected trajectory with eta_t=alpha*D/sqrt(S_(t+1)) and zero-feedback skipping, the regret is bounded by (1/(2alpha)+alpha)*D*sqrt(S_T) minus the terminal squared distance times sqrt(S_T)/(2alpha D). The reusable potential theorem separately permits signed real a_t bounded above by C only at played indices, nonnegative nondecreasing weights and T>0; no terminal bound or lower bound on a_t is added.\[\begin{gathered}S_t=\sum_{i<t}\|g_i\|^2,\quad \eta_t=\frac{\alpha D}{\sqrt{S_{t+1}}},\\ \sum_{t<T}(a_t-a_{t+1})w_t\le(C-a_T)w_{T-1},\\ R_T(u)\le\left(\frac1{2\alpha}+\alpha\right)D\sqrt{S_T}-\frac{\|x_T-u\|^2\sqrt{S_T}}{2\alpha D}.\end{gathered} \begin{gathered}\delta_t=\ell_t(x_t)-\ell_t(u),\quad a_t=\|x_t-u\|^2,\\ \delta_t\le\frac{(a_t-a_{t+1})\sqrt{S_{t+1}}}{2\alpha D}+\frac{\alpha D}{2}\frac{\|g_t\|^2}{\sqrt{S_{t+1}}}\quad(D>0),\\ \sum_{t<T}\frac{\|g_t\|^2}{\sqrt{S_{t+1}}}\le2\sqrt{S_T}.\end{gathered}\]Swipe to read the full formula →
    Intuition
    For D>0, legal supporting planes and projection nonexpansiveness give the one-step distance and energy estimate. Summation by parts controls the distance terms with monotone weights sqrt(S_(t+1))/(2alpha D), retaining the signed terminal term. The shared adaptive-energy sum controls the feedback terms. Empty horizons vanish; D=0 forces feasible played and comparator points to coincide, so it is proved separately without dividing by D. Totalized real division makes the displayed residual zero at D=0. Quantitatively, writing a_t=||x_t-u||^2 and delta_t=ell_t(x_t)-ell_t(u), the D>0 one-step result is delta_t <= (a_t-a_(t+1))*sqrt(S_(t+1))/(2alphaD) + (alphaD/2)*||g_t||^2/sqrt(S_(t+1)). The shared energy theorem gives sum(t<T)||g_t||^2/sqrt(S_(t+1)) <= 2sqrt(S_T), so its scaled contribution is at most alphaD sqrt(S_T). If an inclusive energy is zero, nonnegativity forces the current support to be zero; the totalized 0/0 summand is zero and the actual algorithm skips. No positive-energy premise is added.

    Proof idea
    Set alpha=sqrt(2)/2 in the general theorem and simplify its coefficient to sqrt(2), then discard the nonnegative residual. Independently reuse the shared optimal-step lower bound and positive argmin for the scalar benchmark. In each one-zero case construct arbitrarily small positive upper-bound values; the existing strict-decrease facts rule out attainment. Combine the regret inequality with this infimum identity evaluated on the energy of the actual run. The positive-eta image is nonempty with eta=1. The benchmark GLB proof establishes both a lower bound and the greatest-lower-bound direction using the positive optimizer or arbitrarily small mixed-zero objective values; strict decrease alone is not used to prove the infimum. IsGLB.csInf_eq identifies the infimum and Real.sqrt_mul supplies the sqrt(2) factor under nonnegativity.
    Lean reading notes
    The same actual recursive state supplies played point, selected support and inclusive energy. Source time1..T maps to Lean0..T-1; terminal source x_(T+1) is Lean outputT. The loss is proper extended-real and subdifferentiable on a nonempty closed convex finite-dimensional real inner-product domain; legal feedback is required only at actual played points. Fixed SupportPolicy receives time, the entire strict-past loss functions, the played-point history through the current point, and the current loss; it is not reselected for different futures. OracleLaw is a separate stronger sufficient interface. No executable/measurable policy or universal off-path support law is asserted. Empty horizon, zero energy and D=0 are retained; zero selected feedback explicitly skips the projection update. Separate source-display repair, not a change to the original PDF or regret inequality: for B(eta)=D^2/(2eta)+eta*S/2 on eta>0, the infimum is D*sqrt(S) for every D,S>=0. It is attained exactly when both D,S are positive or both are zero. When both are positive the unique minimizer is D/sqrt(S); when both are zero every positive eta minimizes; when exactly one vanishes the infimum0 is unattained. Theorem4.14 displayed minimum must therefore be read as infimum in the one-zero regimes. S is the energy realized by the single adaptive run, frozen during this scalar comparison; no optimal rerun or clairvoyant learner is asserted. Actual selected compiled VALUE parents: BanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveOSD.source_theorem4_14, BanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveOSD.energy_nonneg, BanditRL.OnlineAdaptiveBenchmark.source_benchmark_value. Parent occurrence is not necessity or a complete transitive graph. Current superseding candidate frontier: the actual causal adaptive OSD recursion, explicit zero-feedback skip, signed weighted-potential estimate, general-alpha terminal-residual regret bound, Eq.(4.4), and Theorem4.14 regret plus separately disclosed infimum/attainment benchmark are locally compiled candidates. Earlier cards and contracts retain their historical open-state wording. Current distinct production/source and canary BODY reviews are accepted-with-explicit-delta; exact integration review, combined root/Tests/full harness, contributor and site/registry checks remain required. This does not close any of the eight Chapter2 required forward containers or certify all six future mathematical claims. Chapter2 remains partial, accepted=false and mandatory proof total=null; Chapters3-16 remain unenumerated/null. Whole Chapters1-16 Goal ACTIVE. No merge, deployment, live-site or retirement claim. Selected external Mathlib facts: IsGLB.csInf_eq and Real.sqrt_mul; the positive-eta image has witness eta=1. Shared scalar lower-bound, positive gap/argmin and uniqueness facts come from BanditRL.OnlineOptimalStep.lower_bound, distance_energy_argmin and optimal_unique; zero_distance_decreases and zero_energy_decreases distinguish unattained mixed-zero boundaries. These are selected supporting-chain facts, not a necessity or full-graph certificate.

    Exact Lean statement 
    theorem source_theorem4_14_infimum (V : Domain (E := E)) (D : ℝ) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (loss : ℕ → E → EReal) (x₁ : E) (p : SupportPolicy (E := E)) (hx₁ : x₁ ∈ V.carrier) (T : ℕ) (hconvex : ∀ t < T, IsConvexExtended (loss t)) (hloss : ∀ t < T, BanditRL.OnlineSubgradientDescent.SubdifferentiableOn V (loss t)) (hlegal : LegalFeedback V (Real.sqrt 2 / 2) D loss x₁ p T) (hdiam : ∀ x ∈ V.carrier, ∀ y ∈ V.carrier, ‖x - y‖ ≤ D) (u : E) (hu : u ∈ V.carrier) : regret V (Real.sqrt 2 / 2) D loss x₁ p u T ≤ D * Real.sqrt (2 * energy V (Real.sqrt 2 / 2) D loss x₁ p T) ∧ D * Real.sqrt (2 * energy V (Real.sqrt 2 / 2) D loss x₁ p T) = Real.sqrt 2 * sInf {b : ℝ | ∃ η : ℝ, 0 < η ∧ b = BanditRL.OnlineOptimalStep.upperBound (D ^ 2) (energy V (Real.sqrt 2 / 2) D loss x₁ p T) η}
